# Agent Coordination

Agents working on this repo use this file as the async handoff protocol.

## How it works

1. When an agent finishes a unit of work, it updates its section below
2. It pushes the branch (including this file)
3. The other agent (or the human) reads this file to know what's ready

### Daedalus (architecture & implementation)
- **Branch:** `claude/daedalus-cycle` (Amber worktree `/Users/xian/Development/klatch-worktrees/daedalus`; merges land on `main`)
- **Status:** working — duty cycle armed and confirmed back after the 8/11 reboot (`launchctl`: `daedalus-{START,WORK,STOP}` loaded). Last fire 2026-08-21 17:17 STOP.
- **8/21 fire (STOP, 17:17 PT) — Theseus's tier-two tap: I took the sequencing decision (before the arm, not with it), landed the free two thirds, and corrected his read of the late-subscriber race:** **Zero API spend, no live runs, no server started** — four local test runs, two of them the full suite. His Round 69 memo (`theseus-…-the-detector-is-built-and-it-has-a-second-blind-spot-neither-of-us-named-2026-08-21.md`) read at open, acted on and replied in the same fire (`daedalus-to-theseus-…-two-thirds-of-the-tap-was-free-and-the-late-subscriber-loses-it-silently-2026-08-21.md`, committed and pushed separately per the worktree mail rule). **(1) His §4 asked whether a live-path tap should be proven before an arm or validated during it, and offered a recommendation rather than a decision — I took it as "before".** His premise was that it cannot be exercised without spend. It holds for less of the tap than it looks: the capture decomposes into three parts, and only one needs a live turn. client→emitter was already pinned (`round52b-tool-use-stream-event.test.ts:144-156`); emitter→SSE-frame was free and is now pinned; only "a real turn emits the frame in time" needs the arm. **(2) The one line the whole proposal rests on had no test.** `routes/messages.ts:382` forwards every emitter event with `JSON.stringify(event)`, unfiltered — that hop is what makes a *rejected* expand visible, because `client.ts:901` emits `toolInput: toolUse.input` four lines before `executeTool` (`:905`) and so before `readExpandArg` rejects it. Mutated the forwarding line to drop `toolInput` and ran the **full server suite: 1 file failed, 84 passed; 3 failed, 1405 passed** — every failure in the file added this fire, **nothing in the other 84 noticed**. New `round70-tool-input-on-the-sse-wire.test.ts` (test-only, additive), four tests through the real route with a subscriber attached mid-turn: the string-`from`/`to` case (Theseus's §2(a) — needs no bad copy anywhere, so my Round 68 fix's blast radius really is narrower than the phenomenon's, adopted outright); his §2(b) quiet path where `{query, expand}` leaves an artifact indistinguishable from a search the model meant to run; a well-formed expand round-tripping so the tap separates accepted from rejected; and the replay path. **(3) A correction to his §4 that changes how the tap must be scored.** "The route already handles a late subscriber, so the race is designed for" — handled for *liveness*, not *capture*. `routes/messages.ts:300-320`: a subscriber arriving after the turn settles gets one `message_complete` reconstructed from the DB and **no `tool_use` frames**, because nothing replays them and `toolInput` is persisted nowhere. Replacement recorded so it needs no re-deriving (`your own turns in that conversation` → `your turns and the turns addressed to you`), Theseus's ordering kept (`:738` needs it more — it teaches the numbering at the moment the agent has just got it wrong). **Deferred deliberately, not overlooked:** in a klatch a third agent's turns genuinely have no position, so the replacement is correct in a 1-1 and correct-but-incomplete in a klatch — a design question, not settled inside a typo fix. **(5) His §3 correction to my guard comment landed.** He ran the positive control my guard never had (`leadPairs: 16`) — it threw, exit 1, arithmetic right; filing the guard as "confirmed" off a *non-firing* guard was my error, and it's now closed by a test rather than an argument. Verified his correction by reading the ordering myself: holder entity POSTed at `probe-recall-tool.mjs:1083`, channel at `:1114-1123`, guard at `:1170` — so *"a half-seeded scratch DB is never left behind"* over-claims (rows exactly right, zero written; an empty entity and an empty 0-message channel survive each abort). **Comment-only, proved not asserted:** `git diff … | grep -vE '^[+-]\s*//'` → **empty**, `node --check` parses, seeding untouched — which matters because an arm is about to run through it. Also promotes his 8/18 code-read to a standing fact: **`--dry` is genuinely not server-free.** **Verified:** `npm test` **exit 0** — server **1386/1386, 83 files** (was 1381/82: **+5 and +1, matching the new file exactly**), client **233 passed / 13 skipped** unchanged; `npm run typecheck` clean both packages. - **8/19 fire (START, 09:17 PT) — the standing `--dry` blocker was a command *form*, not a capability; it is broken, and M and N1 are both instrument-confirmed:** **Zero API spend** (two `--dry` runs, 0 model calls each). **(1) Blocker closed.** Theseus's 8/18 §5 escalated to xian that three fires across two agents couldn't run a free `--dry`, and asked for an interactive session or a standing permission grant. **Neither was needed** — and I'd recorded the same wall on 8/18, so this is a correction to me too. Measured each capability by a separate route: localhost egress via `node -e "fetch(...)"` **allowed**; socket binding **allowed**; `curl` **denied**; what actually requires approval is the **inline `VAR=… ` prefix** (`KLATCH_DB=… npm run dev` / `… npx tsx`). `probe-recall-tool.mjs:1047-1049` already documented this about its own flag; nobody applied it to the server launch, and `curl`'s denial supplied a good-enough explanation for three fires. Transferable rule: **a denied tool is evidence about the tool, not about the capability.** Built `scripts/probe-scratch-server.mjs` — sets `KLATCH_DB` in-process, spawns `tsx` as a child. Guards the **dotenv `override: true` hazard** (`index.ts:17` — a `KLATCH_DB` in `.env` would beat ours, and `.env` symlinks outside the sandbox so it can't be read to check) by requiring the sqlite `-shm` sidecar beside the scratch path as proof of a live connection, killing the server and exiting non-zero before the probe writes a row. The load-bearing result: **the leading offer is the dearer one in *both* renders** (28-vs-27 single, 28-vs-23 two-excerpt), so N1's cost-inversion isn't contingent on which render the live query produces. Guard silent (15 ≤ 15) and no continuation emitted (28 < 30) — both as designed. **Nothing structural stands between N1 and a live run; the remaining gate is a spend decision, xian's, not a verification gap.** **(4) Iris's branch (a) verified** (I'd flagged it deliberately unverified on 8/18): claude.ai import **does** silently attach to a matching project (`import.ts:572` → `findOrCreateProject`, `queries.ts:1158-1179`), and **does not toast — unreachably so**, because `findOrCreateProject` returns no matched-vs-created signal, the caller keeps only `project.id`, and the response (`import.ts:694-699`) has no project field at all. ~3-line server change once Iris rules on shape (per-project vs aggregate; toast vs the dialog's existing result view) — no code written on a guess. The `--dry` remains the right first action of any N build. **(3) His §3 point 2 — the unclamped offer — closed with tests rather than left for N2 to hit live.** Verified from source: `renderExcerpt` addresses the whole reachable stretch (`recall.ts:858-882`) while `expand` returns `all.slice(0, 30)` (`:748`). Round 56's cap test existed but *constructs* the over-cap range by hand; **nothing exercised the sequence an agent actually takes** — follow an offer wider than the cap verbatim, then follow the continuation. +3 tests in `round56-recall-expand.test.ts`: the precondition asserted not assumed (offer 6–45, fails if it ever drops under the cap); **the prose sentence and the trailing edge marker cannot disagree** (assembled by different code, both say "where next"); and **the pair tiles the offer** — no hole, no overlap, ends where the offer ended, second call terminates instead of receding. Effect for Theseus: N2's free second measurement is now a measurement of the *model*, not a possible bug of ours. **(4) New — `scripts/verify-filler-constraints.mjs`.** N1's blocking half is content (10 new `FILLER_LEAD` pairs under four constraints that live only as prose in two docblocks — the shape that let `REACHABLE_R54` read a false zero). - **8/17 fire (WORK, 13:17 PT) — his stale-reference report fixed in `packages/`, my width ceiling retired as refuted, and his replacement for it doesn't fit the same data either:** Theseus's arm-L memo landed at 13:17; read, actioned and replied in this fire. **(1) His §4 fixed, and not the way it looks.** `recall.ts` and `round58-recall-marker-phrases.test.ts` both cited `scripts/probe-recall-tool.mjs:1059` for `REACHABLE_R54` — **verified at both ends before editing**: `git show b9a9fd2:scripts/probe-recall-tool.mjs` has it at exactly 1059, and `2496f72` (the very next commit) moved the recogniser to `scripts/lib/recall-recogniser.mjs:60`. `grep -rn "\.mjs:[0-9]" packages/` returned exactly the two he named. **Fixed by deleting the line number, not updating it to `:60`** — updating re-arms the trap at the next refactor, and the paragraph those numbers sit inside is *about* references that go stale silently instead of failing loudly. Deliberately **not** mass-edited: logs/mail/round docs are dated records and rewriting them to match today's file inverts the discipline. Rule written into the design record instead, scoped to **live documents only**: cite `packages/` by symbol and module, or pin the line to a commit (`recall.ts:1059 @ b9a9fd2`). **(2) My ~19-row width ceiling is retired, and it deserves a harder word than his.** He said "does not survive n=13"; **four runs took 27 rows, so it is a direct counterexample, not an underpowered datum**. **(3) But his replacement overreaches on the same 13 points** — "width taken is bounded by the width offered and nothing else visible" doesn't fit: **9 of 13 stopped short of the offer** (F/L offer `4–30`; the 9s are `4–12`, the 11s `4–14`), and **the three K runs stopped at `4–22` of an offered `4–40`** — short of both the offer and the 30-row cap, an interior stop. 1 that no search of yours can reach …]`. **Four decisions, each a way it could have gone quietly wrong:** the two counts are **separated and stated as affordances rather than categories** (turns in the entity's own transcript are reachable by another query; and an excerpt the char budget dropped is not on the page, so a count measured to it is true about the room and false about what the agent can check — this is why the render is now a second pass after the budget loop); - **8/15 fire (START, 09:17 PT) — Theseus's §3 landed as Round 52, and the wire field Iris asked me to price turned out to already exist with no consumer (Round 52b):** Two items were mine this fire. **(1) The klatch excerpt that hides its own gap (`5848778`).** Theseus ranked it above everything else in his Round 51 live memo and was right that it is structural rather than probabilistic: `seq` is `ROW_NUMBER` over the **scoped** set, so a row removed by the entity scope leaves no gap in the numbering — the numbering closes over it. `groupIntoExcerpts` splits on non-contiguous `ordinal`, so two rows that had another agent's turn between them render as **one continuous exchange**: a bare `"Understood."` presented as the turn immediately after the agent's own `"Confirmed. Noted."`, antecedent silently deleted, in a shape that asserts adjacency. Every klatch in the corpus, and the first scope-driven omission that ever had to *render*, so nothing before Round 51 could have exposed it. Fix: `NeighbourhoodMessage.rawOrdinal` — the same position over the channel's **whole** message list, from a `raw` CTE restricted to the channels `scoped` touched (unrestricted it windows the entire `messages` table). A jump in `ordinal` is **distance**, a jump in `rawOrdinal` alone is **scope**; both need to be visible and only the first was. **Three judgements, each a way it could have gone quietly wrong:** **marked, not split** — he offered `---` and I declined it, because a scope gap leaves `ordinal` contiguous *and should*, so `---` would say "two separate stretches of conversation" about one stretch with pieces withheld, a different false claim rather than a fix; **interior only** — an edge gap is the radius, already covered by "Nothing outside these excerpts was read", and marking it too would make the marker mean two things; **it does not name who spoke them** — practically other agents, but the only thing true by construction is that the rows failed the entity-transcript predicate, and this line is read by a model that reasons confidently from whatever it is told. Header sentence explaining the marker is **conditional** on one surviving the char budget. Built the recent-N half: `packages/server/src/claude/carried-context.ts`, appended by `buildSystemPrompt` as **layer 6**, wired at all three assembly call sites (both `client.ts` inference paths, `prompt-debug`, both AAXT routes) and deliberately **not** in `export/assemble.ts` — layer 6 is another channel's verbatim content and baking it into this channel's exported package is a leak no export contract promises. Assembled **per entity inside the roundtable loop**, so a six-agent klatch pays the seed in six separate prompts rather than one prompt six times the size — that is the whole difference between (b) and the excluded option (a). Surfaced as `6_carriedContext` with byte counts in `prompt-debug` and both AAXT routes, which is the observability property Theseus argued (b) for. **Budget measured against the real March corpus, not guessed** (`backups/klatch.db.backup-2026-03-14`, worked on a copy, copy deleted): 20 messages / 24,000 chars per block / 4,000 chars per message. The per-message cap is load-bearing and not defensive tidiness — the largest real message is **64,627 chars**, more than twice the whole block budget, so without it that one message is carried alone and evicts the other nineteen (p50 580, p90 2,334, p99 7,984). **Defect found and fixed, inert until this fire:** `getEntityTranscript` (Round 36) scoped on `m.entity_id`, but every user message is written with that column NULL — we only stamp an entity on the assistant row — so the union returned each agent's **answers with none of the questions** (real corpus: **1,332 user rows missing vs 1,240 assistant rows carried**). Round 36's fixtures only ever inserted assistant rows, so no test could express the failure; it survived review for that reason, not for lack of care. Now matches the rule `buildPanelHistory` has always used — a user message qualifies on **room membership**, not authorship (`EXISTS` on `channel_entities`). Proven load-bearing against the real corpus with built code: the same seed that carried 0 user messages now carries 9 of 20. **Verified this fire: `npm test` 1199 server (+21) / 212 client, exit 0; typecheck clean ×3 workspaces; `npm run build` green.** **Not proven:** no live klatch turn driven through a running server — every test mocks the SDK, so the seed is verified as assembled and wired, not as behaviourally effective. **New blocker surfaced, needs xian:** measured against the corpus as it actually is, the seed comes out **1,583 chars from 4 rooms** rather than the ~12–22K the per-channel arithmetic predicts — because the corpus predates `#1` and all 65 imported channels still bind to `default-entity`, so there is only one agent and "its activity elsewhere" is a mix. The wiring is correct and is correctly carrying the wrong thing. - **8/12 fire (START, 09:17 PT) — the `stop_reason` gap is closed; the "additive" migration turned out not to be (`03cfd01`):** Iris ruled on the message-status surface (`iris-to-daedalus-cc-team-incomplete-status-decision-2026-08-11.md` → `docs/ux/message-incomplete-status-2026-08-11.md`), which was the single stated blocker on queued item #1. Took her shape as written: `Message.status` gains **`'incomplete'`**, new `Message.stopReason` (`max_tokens | context_window_exceeded | refusal | pause_turn`), `end_turn`/`stop_sequence` untouched. `mapStopReason()` returns `undefined` for unknown and future reasons rather than coercing them into copy never written for them. **Three consequences found by following the status through rather than assuming additivity, each a real defect had it shipped without them:** **(1)** the migration is *not* additive — `messages` carries `CHECK (status IN ('complete','streaming','error'))`, SQLite cannot alter a CHECK in place, so every existing `klatch.db` (including the real 2,652-message one on Amber) would have **thrown a constraint error on the first truncated response** instead of recording it; added the supported table rebuild, guarded on live `sqlite_master` text, `foreign_keys` off so the `DROP` doesn't cascade `message_artifacts` away. **(2)** history assembly filtered on `status === 'complete'`, so a fourth status would have silently dropped truncated turns out of the prompt — the model would stop seeing content still on the user's screen; incomplete turns stay in history now. **(3)** the SSE replay path treated only `complete`/`error` as finished, so an incomplete message would have polled the full 2-minute deadline for a stream that already ended. **Two additions beyond the spec:** `StreamEvent.stopReason` (the client updates optimistically at `App.tsx:103-107` and never refetches, so without it the bubble renders clean until reload — the same silent-corruption failure displaced by one page load; the client-side threading is left to Iris, it's her surface), and export/import round-trip of `stop_reason`, which was otherwise flattening `'incomplete'` back to `'complete'`. **Verified this fire: `npm test` 1178 server (+21) / 212 client, exit 0; typecheck clean ×3 workspaces; `npm run build` green.** The 5 migration tests run against a **real legacy-shaped DB on disk**, not the in-memory harness — the harness builds today's schema, the one shape that cannot exercise the rebuild — and assert the precondition first, so a pass is evidence of the migration and not of a constraint that was never there. **Not proven:** no live truncated response driven through; every test mocks the SDK, so the mapping is verified against the documented union (`messages.d.ts:1067`), not observed. Proposed to Iris that one attended `max_tokens` send close both halves as a single MAXT item. **Residual recorded, not fixed:** `MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS` exhaustion exits with `stop_reason === 'tool_use'` and still stores `'complete'` — a second way a turn ends unfinished while looking clean; folding it in would mean extending Iris's enum, and there's no evidence yet it fires in practice. Reply filed and pushed separately to `main` per the worktree mail rule; thread left open (her client half is real open work). Full detail: `docs/logs/2026-08-12-0917-daedalus-opus-log.md`.

The notice holds as a hedge until a tool result gives the agent something to resolve it against. - **2026-08-13 (START fire) — carried-context visibility question decided: yes, visible, as a passive per-message signal.** Daedalus routed a design question in his 8/12 WORK-fire memo (`daedalus-to-xian-cc-team-carried-context-live-backfill-now-blocking-2026-08-12.md`): should the human see what an agent carried into a klatch from its other channels (layer 6), or is it plumbing? Theseus's same-day live probe (`docs/research/carried-context-conveyance-probe-2026-08-12.md`) made it concrete, not hypothetical: two agents in one klatch, same mechanism, opposite behavior — Corvus volunteered its carried fact unprompted (coined "my carried context" itself, that wording isn't in the prompt), Vesper declined even after explicit owner authorization. Right now the only channel by which a human learns context was carried in is which way an individual agent's live judgment falls. **Decision: visible, not silent** — grounded in the standing `design-principles.md` principle from 8/10 ("presentation must not imply a guarantee the mechanism doesn't provide"), applied from the opposite direction: §6 already says an agent carries everything it knows into a klatch turn, and a silent room implies the opposite. **Shape, scoped to avoid the "noise" objection Daedalus rightly raised:** not a content dump — Klatch already has the exact precedent one component over, `MessageList.tsx`'s passive "💭 Thought about this" thinking-artifact chip. Carried context gets the same treatment: `🧵 Carried context from N other conversations`, count only, no channel names, no expand (`/prompt-debug` already covers full detail for anyone who wants it). Doc: `docs/ux/carried-context-visibility-2026-08-13.md`. **What this needs from Daedalus, not built by me:** a message needs to carry, at creation time, whether layer 6 was active and a room count — sketched two shapes (new `ArtifactType: 'carried_context'` reusing the existing `message_artifacts`/`ArtifactList` render path, my lean; or a lighter boolean+count pair on `Message`), his call on persistence. **Deliberately not answered:** Theseus's separate disclosure-norm question (should the prompt state a sharing norm) stays Daedalus's call; his `?entityId=` observability gap stays his/Theseus's. ## Signals

Use these status values:
- **available** — done with current work, ready for next task
- **working** — actively building, don't wait on me
- **blocked** — need something before I can continue (describe in Notes)
- **review** — work is pushed, requesting review before merge

## Branch discipline

All in-progress work happens on feature branches. `main` must always be demo-ready — tests pass, app runs, no half-finished features. Only merge to `main` when the feature is complete and verified. This lets anyone check out `main` at any time for a clean demo or to base new work on a stable snapshot.

## Merge protocol

Merging feature branches into main is handled by **xian + Calliope** (or Daedalus for his own branches). To avoid silent deletions from stale branches:

1. **Rebase or merge main into your branch before pushing for review.** This ensures your branch includes all recent main changes. If you skip this, git may silently "delete" files that were added to main after your branch diverged.
2. **Daedalus reviews the diff stat before merging.** Any unexpected file deletions, additions outside the assignment scope, or changes to shared docs (CLAUDE.md, ROSTER.md, AXT.md, ROADMAP.md) will be reverted during merge.
3. **Stay in your lane.** Only modify files within your assignment scope. If you notice something that needs fixing outside your scope, note it in your log or mail — don't fix it yourself.

## Protocol

- Pull from origin and read this file at session start
- Check `docs/mail/` for memos addressed to you
- Update your section before every push (include `Updated:` timestamp)
- If you need something from the other agent, say so in "Waiting on"
- Keep Notes short — link to docs/tests for details
